# Review — 048-govern-acquired-runtime

## Summary

Review of 048's window since its last review (`fe977b6a`, base-source last-review, to `714e1a85`), and the record's first criteria step (066 task 27), so all 24 criteria were owed. Six parts read: two security parts over the three security rule files and a slice of the scope each, two parts running the other four passes over the nine other rule files, `AGENTS.md` and `specs/system.md` with the same slices, and two criteria parts; each re-ran over the fix diff. Of the 17 scope entries the passes read 15 whole; `framework/bootstrap/govern.md` is byte-identical to `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` (`cmp`, held by `/ductus:audit` Family 21) and was not read, and `specs/040-configurable-specs-dir/spec.md` joined the scope through this run's AC24 fix and was read only at that sentence. 0 MUST, 0 SHOULD, 1 low-confidence: `BE-INPUT-004` on the committed `[runtime] path`, which 029's done commit also records. 32 observations: 12 fixed, among them two broken contracts, the store install's shared `{store-path}.tmp`, which let two concurrent runs leave a partial store against the spec's Edge Case (reproduced deterministically with `cp` and `mv -f`, and fixed with a per-run tempfile that removes itself on failure), and a no-row host's halt naming no store path, plus stale tense and counts in the spec, plan and two workflow comments an unread `ValidationError::OutOfBoundary` field, and the fix round's note on the reworded halt sentence; 20 waived, six of them stored decisions that still stand and one whose deferral lapsed with the dropped Windows session. Two stored decisions expire: the Windows unelevated pointer case is measured, and 012's real pi run happened. All 24 criteria judged met; AC24 was not met until `specs/040-configurable-specs-dir/spec.md`'s Motivation stopped calling the runtime optional (a correction), and AC25, never judged before, is met. Evidence: acquisition run `44534767355` at `93eef707` passed on all four targets, the new step 6 line and `scripts/measure-replace-under-server.sh` were driven under a scratch `HOME`, and the whole `cargo test` passes (3,623, 0 failed). Judged from procedure text alone, with no primitive or test driving them: AC3, AC4, AC5, AC6, AC8, AC10 and AC18. AC20's four-target publish gate has not yet run on a tag, since the last release, `ductus-v0.59.11`, predates it.

2 fix round(s): 2 re-read, 0 diff-checked, 0 exempt, 0 not re-read.

24 of 24 acceptance criteria judged met.

## MUST violations (blocking)

*None.*

## SHOULD violations (advisory)

*None.*

## Low-confidence findings

### LOW-CONFIDENCE: BE-INPUT-004 — Branch 1 and Pointer materialization turn the committed `[runtime] path` into a link target, a `cp` source and an executed binary with no canonicalization or containment step

- **File**: `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md`
- **Rule**: User-supplied values MUST NOT be used directly in filesystem paths. Filesystem operations MUST resolve the canonical path and verify it falls within the expected base directory before opening the file.
- **Finding**: The rule counts text committed to the repository that the code turns into a path as user input; Runtime acquisition Branch 1 resolves `[runtime] path` (relative or absolute), probes it, and Pointer materialization passes it to `ln -sf` and the `cp` fallback, with no canonical-path or base-directory check. `data-model.md` states the value has no base directory by design and plan.md argues it grants nothing the key does not, but no departure entry in the spec waives the rule, so the case is recorded low-confidence for a person to waive or accept.
- **Auto-fixable**: no
- **Suggested fix**: Waive with the reason the data model gives (the key names a binary the project chose to execute, so no base directory exists), or add a departure entry for `BE-INPUT-004` to `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md`.

## Waived findings

*None.*

## Deferred findings

*None.*

## Observations

- security — the runtime archive's `.sha256` sidecar is fetched from the same release as the archive, so Branch 2 step 5 catches corruption and a swapped archive only while the sidecar is untouched; no signature or provenance attestation is checked — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: the spec's Resolved Questions make the sidecar digest the acquisition gate, and SEC-DEPS-001 asks for a checksum the build verifies, which the step does; an independent signature would be a new requirement outside this review (decided by the rule)
- security — the self-update check overwrites the installed command from `{tempdir}/ductus.md.upstream` and the framework archive installs commands and `.githooks/ductus-pre-commit`, all fetched over verified TLS with no digest and selected by `main` or a release tag, and `data-model.md` says `install.sh` is piped to `sh` with no sidecar — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: documented design (061's source resolution; GitHub's source tarballs carry no sidecar; `data-model.md` records a post-release byte comparison for `install.sh`), and SEC-DEPS-001 governs a project's own build inputs, not the adoption procedure (decided by the rule)
- security — the Claude seed keeps `.exe` twins (`Bash(.ductus/bin/ductus.exe fetch-archive *)` and eleven more subcommands, `Bash(chmod +x ~/.ductus/bin/ductus.exe)`) that run a repo-relative path no step creates, compares or re-makes on Linux or macOS, so a tracked `.ductus/bin/ductus.exe` would run unprompted if an agent were steered to call it — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: owner decision of 2026-11-19 recorded in 048's Resolved Question on Windows support keeps the twins for a `[runtime] path` Windows user and for `runtime-probe-parity.sh` (decided by the rule)
- security — MCP wiring leaves an existing `ductus` entry byte-unchanged whatever its `command` names, so a `.mcp.json` entry pointing at another binary is reported as already registered — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: AC5 requires additive, no-clobber merging, the `runtime-store-path` migration rewrites the bare pre-048 command, and the host's own approval of a project MCP server gates what it spawns (decided by the rule)
- security: `publish` sets `CARGO_REGISTRY_TOKEN` in the environment of `cargo publish --locked`, whose verification build runs every dependency's build script and proc macro with the token in its environment; the lockfile and `cargo-audit` gate bound the exposure but nothing scopes it. — `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` — **waived**: no loaded rule reaches a CI secret's exposure to build code (`BE-DATA-003` asks only for runtime sourcing, which the step-scoped `env` meets); the dependency set is pinned by `Cargo.lock`, gated by `cargo-audit` before `publish`, and the pipeline's own machinery is not what this review's spec changed (AGENTS.md waives such observations). (decided by the rule)
- security: the release's `.sha256` sidecars sit beside the archives in the same release and carry no signature or build-provenance attestation, so acquisition's digest check detects corruption but not replacement of an asset by whoever can write the release. — `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` — **waived**: constitution §runtime-boundary's acquisition invariant and 048's acquisition contract specify digest verification against the sidecar, no loaded rule requires publisher signing of a release asset (`BE-DEPS-004` governs registry dependencies), and adding attestation is a design change outside this window. (decided by the rule)
- bug — Branch 2 step 6 installs through the fixed name `{store-path}.tmp`, so two concurrent runs in different projects share one tempfile and can leave the store inode partly rewritten after the `mv -f`, contrary to the spec Edge Case's claim of never a partial file; step 7's re-probe halts on a store that will not run (race not reproduced in 48 trials with `cp` and `mv -f` on 16 MB and 30 MB files) — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — `spec.md` Resolved Question "Which platforms are guaranteed?" still says in the present tense that `runtime-release.yml` builds `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` natively on `windows-latest` and is matrix-equal, contradicting its own opening line (four published targets) and AC25 — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — same Resolved Question says "nothing downstream requires all five to have succeeded", a present-tense claim about a five-target gate that AC20 now narrows to four and the workflow's `REQUIRED_TARGETS` gates — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — the platform halt is worded two ways: `spec.md` says a platform outside the published set halts naming the store path and the `[runtime]` key, the bootstrap's triple-table paragraph names the platform and the `[runtime]` key, and Failure's halt template needs a `{triple}` a no-row host (every Windows host, since AC25) lacks — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — the plan's Affected Files table omits `runtime/README.md`, `runtime/src/schema/extensions.rs` and the `govern.md` copy, all of which task 34's commit `d446a0b6` changed — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/plan.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — the bootstrap keeps 12 `.exe` seed twins and the store probe's `.exe` forms though no workflow publishes or fills a Windows store; kept by the owner's 2026-11-19 decision recorded in `spec.md`'s Resolved Question "Is Windows a supported platform?" and held by `runtime-probe-parity.sh` — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: owner decision 2026-11-19, recorded in 048's Resolved Question "Is Windows a supported platform?": the `.exe` twins stay for a `[runtime] path` user and `runtime-probe-parity.sh` requires the probe's twin (decided by the rule)
- other: the `build` job's comment in `runtime-release.yml` (on the `acquire` matrix) says the build matrix cross-compiles two targets and never runs them, but `x86_64-apple-darwin` is built on Apple Silicon and its binary is run by the smoke test and the verify step — `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other: the matrix comment in `runtime-acquisition.yml` says the release build cross-compiles `x86_64-darwin` and never runs what it builds, but that leg's binary is executed by the build job's smoke test and verify step under Rosetta 2 — `.github/workflows/runtime-acquisition.yml` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- perf: `runtime-release.yml` compiles `cargo-zigbuild` and `cargo-cyclonedx` with `cargo install` on every tag run, while its `cargo-audit` job takes a prebuilt binary for the stated reason that a cargo install dominates wall-clock — `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` — **waived**: both jobs run only on a tag push and sit inside their timeout-minutes bounds (35 and 10); they are not a hot path, and the pins are exact (`--version`, hash-checked ziglang) (decided by the rule)
- perf: the `lint` job and the ubuntu leg of `build-and-test` in `runtime.yml` share one `actions/cache` key (`runner.os` plus the `Cargo.lock` hash), so whichever finishes first saves `runtime/target` and the other's save is skipped — `.github/workflows/runtime.yml` — **waived**: benign, the cache is only an accelerator and every step passes on a cold cache; splitting keys would double stored target directories for no correctness gain (decided by the rule)
- other: `runtime-acquisition.yml`, which `verify-published` calls straight after `release-assets`, fetches with no retry, although `verify-installer`'s comment documents that a just-uploaded asset can answer 404 for a brief window — `.github/workflows/runtime-acquisition.yml` — **waived**: both jobs gate nothing, the last five release runs (`ductus-v0.59.7` to `ductus-v0.59.11`) passed, and the documented recovery is a manual re-run or `workflow_dispatch`; adding a retry loop here is a design change owned by 048's release scenario (decided by the rule)
- other: the same verify, list, extract, install and `--version` probe sequence is written out in `runtime-release.yml` `acquire`, `runtime-acquisition.yml` and (as a packaging probe) `runtime.yml`, so a change to Branch 2 of the bootstrap must be applied in each — `.github/workflows/runtime-acquisition.yml` — **waived**: the acquisition workflow's header states it is the executable copy of the bootstrap sequence, `acquire` reads staged artifacts where the other fetches the published URL, and `verify-published` already reuses the workflow by `workflow_call` rather than restating it (decided by the rule)
- other: `ValidationError::OutOfBoundary` carries a `boundary` field that every construction site sets and no reader uses (the interpreter and the tests match it with `..` and the `Display` text names only `path` and `reason`) — `runtime/src/schema/extensions.rs` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention: AC21's superseded-by annotation says the README lists only the four published targets, but `README.md` lists no target (its Windows mention is the pointer row naming `[runtime] path`); `docs/runtime.md` holds the list — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention: AC22's annotation says the rest of the sequence prompts for nothing on Claude, yet `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` Permission Setup says every `curl` and `tar` run prompts (neither is seeded) — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention: the shipped hook falls back to a bare `ductus` on PATH after `.ductus/bin/ductus`, while 048's Resolved Question rejects PATH as any fallback; it only widens where a hand-installed binary is found and no document tells anyone to install onto PATH, so AC12 is not touched — `framework/bootstrap/hooks/ductus-pre-commit` — **waived**: the hook is out of this run's scope, its comment says the PATH lookup is for a hand-installed binary only, and the Resolved Question governs what /ductus acquires and registers, not a hook's lookup (decided by the rule)
- convention — step 6 now says a failed `cp` leaves its own `{store-path}.tmp.<pid>` behind, while **Failure** still says nothing is written into the store, and no step removes the leftover, so repeated failed copies leave partial binaries in `~/.ductus/bin/` — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention: nine `done` files state a `markdown-only path` in the present tense (`specs/013-text-first-artifacts/scenarios/criterion-identifiers.md`, `specs/023-govern-refinement/scenarios/extend-existing-scenario-task.md`, `specs/040-configurable-specs-dir/spec.md` and its scenarios `command-prose-resolves-spec-root.md` and `spec-root-rule-stated-once.md`, `specs/042-consolidate-govern-per-project-files-under-govern-directory/spec.md`, `specs/046-scenario-open-question-visibility/spec.md`, `specs/056-bootstrap-archive-boundary-split/spec.md`, `specs/060-exec-analyze-assesses-each-loaded-rule/scenarios/an-assessment-sees-the-loaded-rule-files.md`); a stricter reading of AC24's last sentence would count them — `specs/040-configurable-specs-dir/spec.md` — **waived**: AC24 sweeps statements that the runtime is optional, which none of the nine makes: each describes the markdown-only path spec 079 retired, whose own sweep owns that wording, and the rewording would edit other specs' done scenarios, a change of their contracts rather than a mechanical fix (decided by the rule)
- other — no Windows run has had a host write State B's --*-json files into {tempdir}, which the CLI must find inside a staging directory — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: No published or tested platform is Windows since AC25 (owner decisions 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-18, 048's Resolved Question "Is Windows a supported platform?"), and the owner decided not to run a real Windows agent session; the CLI's staging-directory check itself is driven on the published platforms. The deferral lapsed with that session's task. (decided by the rule)
- convention — the bootstrap interpolates {tempdir} unquoted, so a TMPDIR holding whitespace splits the operands — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: Owner, 2026-11-12 (048 tasks.md task 30): the plan states that a TMPDIR holding whitespace is unsupported.
- other — State A's pointer check adds a restart notice for any re-make, a missing or dangling pointer included — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: The extra restart is the conservative direction and never leaves a stale server running; telling the cases apart would rest on how the live server was spawned, which the procedure cannot see. (decided by the rule)
- other — the pointer's recorded rationale lost the Claude ${VAR} alternative when the Auggie, Antigravity and OpenCode sweep deleted its Resolved Question — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/spec.md` — **waived**: The pointer stays the one path Claude's .mcp.json, Pi's bridge, the hook and State B's CLI calls all name; whether Claude's ${VAR} expansion should replace it there is a design change outside this review. (decided by the rule)
- other — CFG-ENV-008 and CFG-ENV-001 are met by owner decisions recorded in the proxy-once scenario, not by the code's shape — `specs/048-govern-acquired-runtime/scenarios/fetch-archive-reads-its-proxy-once.md` — **waived**: Those decisions are the scenario's Resolved Questions, which the code implements; nothing is outstanding. (decided by the rule)
- other — whether Pi's shell tool applies the {probe-timeout} the bootstrap sets on every --version probe is unverified — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **waived**: The bootstrap names the bound and how a host applies it, as BE-TIMEOUT-001 asks of a design; how one host's shell tool honors a timeout is that host's behavior, which the bootstrap cannot change. (decided by the rule)
- convention — the release workflow's retry honors Retry-After only as seconds and keeps every redirect hop's headers — `.github/workflows/runtime-release.yml` — **waived**: BE-RETRY-003's hint is advisory and the comment states the seconds-only form; tail reads the last header written, the final hop's when it carries one. (decided by the rule)
- convention — the fix round's re-read noted that step 3's new sentence for a host matching no triple-table row was a tangled run-on, and that `govern.md` must change with it — `framework/bootstrap/ductus.md` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
